# de-non-resident-tax: figure and rule ledger

Guide: [de-non-resident-tax](../skills/international/germany/de-non-resident-tax.md). Accepted Guide SHA-256: `2240579d7a85e98bbc9f6994ccd0a9a2d99025d4f1fa8c6ed94b18c7e7a25174`.
Repackaged from the completed [independent country review](https://github.com/openaccountants/openaccountants/blob/bceaf70083a8c8573ba8558a339ab11b1b4095bf/ledgers/germany-2026-evidence/independent-review/report.md) at `bceaf70083a8c8573ba8558a339ab11b1b4095bf`. The Guide bytes, all current ledger claims and all original-topic destinations are preserved. This is a packaging change; it does not claim a new legal review or accountant attestation.
Original published ledger SHA-256: `9dec795fd8521beff794535f0c9522fae22c79ce28da8ac228408e77314e0438`. Full source captures, earlier versions, scripts and review chains remain in the original archive; none is added by this replacement. Source excerpts below retain their actual read dates. Short excerpts identify the previously reviewed operative unit; they do not replace its complete conditions.

| 001 | Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026 LF[18, 18] | This skill decides whether a person is taxed in Germany on worldwide income or only on German income, then works out how the German income is taxed: by assessment, by wage tax, or by a flat withholding. It also covers the reverse case: a German resident with foreign income who wants credit for the foreign tax. | [NR.RES](#q-2b4f96a4017359), [NR.NR](#q-9fc924491a99c8), [NR.OPTION13](#q-b3de798a40f558). | 011 | Who this applies to LF[33, 33] | **Unlimited liability (§ 1(1) EStG).** A person with a residence (Wohnsitz) or habitual abode (gewöhnlicher Aufenthalt) in Germany is taxed on worldwide income. | [NR.RES](#q-2b4f96a4017359). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 012 | Who this applies to LF[35, 35] | - **Residence (§ 8 AO):** a home the person keeps and uses, in circumstances showing they will keep and use it. | 013 | Who this applies to LF[36, 36] | - **Habitual abode (§ 9 AO):** staying in a way that shows the stay is not just temporary. A continuous stay of more than six months always counts, from the first day; short breaks are ignored. The six-month rule does not apply to a purely private stay (visit, holiday, cure) of up to one year (§ 9 sentence 3 AO). | [NR.ABODE](#q-d39533a0251e82). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |

| 019 | Who this applies to LF[46, 46] | German income that a tax treaty limits to a reduced rate (for example dividends) counts as not subject to German tax for the test (§ 1(3) sentence 3). The foreign income must be shown by a certificate from the foreign tax authority (§ 1(3) sentence 5). § 50a withholding still applies (§ 1(3) sentence 6). | [NR.OPTION13](#q-b3de798a40f558). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 020 | Who this applies to LF[48, 48] | **§ 1a EStG, EU/EEA nationals.** An EU or EEA national who is fully liable under § 1(1) or treated so under § 1(3) can have a spouse who lives in another EU/EEA state treated as fully liable, so the couple gets joint assessment and splitting. For the § 1(3) test the income of both spouses counts and the Grundfreibetrag is doubled to EUR 24,696 (§ 1a(1) no. 2). A non-EU/EEA national under § 1(3) gets the Grundfreibetrag but not splitting. | [NR.SPOUSE](#q-4c4cf941f4974f), [NR.COUPLE](#q-6e4a6e2b4bf4b3). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 021 | Who this applies to LF[50, 50] | **Companies.** A company with neither its seat nor its place of management in Germany has limited corporate tax liability on its German income (§ 2 no. 1 KStG). Income settled by withholding is final unless it belongs to a German business (§ 32(1) KStG). A German permanent establishment is assessed at the 15% corporate rate (§ 23 KStG; see de-corporate-tax). | [NR.COMPANYNR](#q-c562bba0c9b5c6), [NR.COMPFINAL](#q-9c235e9c0127ac), [NR.CORPRATE](#q-6c34e4cce443ab). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |

| 055 | Step 2: is the tax settled by withholding? (§ 50(2) EStG) LF[102, 108] | - German employment income under § 49(1) no. 4 where a § 39a(4) allowance was entered and annual pay exceeds the Grundfreibetrag plus the employee and special-expense lump sums (no. 4(a)); an application under § 46(2) no. 8 (no. 4(b)); or the § 46(2) nos. 2, 5 and 5a cases (no. 4(c));<br>- employment applications connected with extraordinary income under § 34(1) and (2) nos. 2 and 4 (no. 4(d)), or an application for a foreign-tax credit under § 50d(7) sentence 2 (no. 4(e)); these have their own gates, rather than no. 4(b)'s nationality/residence restriction;<br>- income under § 50a(1) nos. 1, 2 and 4 on application (no. 5; assessed by the BZSt);<br>- a later finding that the conditions for full liability under § 1(2), § 1(3) or § 1a were not met (no. 2), or the mixed-year case under § 2(7) sentence 3 (no. 3);<br>- the specified life-insurance capital income under § 49(1) no. 5 sentence 1(a), to which § 20(1) no. 6 sentence 2 applies, on application (no. 6).<br><br>The EU/EEA nationality and residence restriction in § 50(2) sentence 7 applies to no. 4(b) and no. 5. Sentence 8 additionally extends no. 4(b) to the specified EU/EEA nationals resident in Switzerland and Swiss nationals resident in the EU or Switzerland. It is not a general restriction on no. 4(d), no. 4(e) or no. 6. For no. 4 employment cases use the competent employer's Betriebsstättenfinanzamt under sentences 3–6; for no. 6 use the debtor's income-tax office under sentence 10. The referred special-tariff, insurance and credit computations are not supplied by this assessment-gate list. A person actually qualifying for full liability under § 1(3) or § 1a follows that full-liability route instead. | [NR.FINAL](#q-5ff77755fd5d53), [R20.assessment-exceptions](#q-0ec83656e10921). | 063 | Step 3: the assessment of a non-resident (§ 50(1) EStG) LF[118, 118] | 5. **Solidarity surcharge.** Establish the assessment base under § 3 SolzG; it is not automatically the unadjusted income-tax amount. Above that threshold, take the smaller of 5.5% of the base and 11.9% of the excess over EUR 20,350, dropping fractions of a cent (§§ 3(3), 4 SolzG). | 076 | Kapitalertragsteuer on dividends to non-residents LF[146, 146] | The German company withholds 25% plus 5.5% surcharge, 26.375% in all, whoever the shareholder is. For a non-resident shareholder without a German business, that settles the German tax. The excess over the treaty rate is reclaimed from the BZSt under § 50c(3). Example: a treaty limit of 15% gives a refund of 26.375% − 15% = 11.375% of the gross dividend. For a capital-income creditor that is a corporation subject to limited liability within § 2 no. 1 KStG, § 44a(9) provides a refund of two fifths of the Kapitalertragsteuer actually withheld and remitted. This incorporates the application/procedure in § 50c(3) and (5) and the anti-abuse test and exceptions in § 50d(3); it is not an unconditional refund for every foreign company. Further statutory or treaty relief claims remain separate. The treaty rate itself depends on the treaty; see de-treaty-withholding-rates for the rates by country. | [NR.KWHT](#q-a807ae44f25753), [NR.WHTSOLI](#q-348ed33c6ae242), [NR.REFUND](#q-074a70562a74a7), [NR.REFUND40](#q-0e8aafc74a4a98), [R19.refund](#q-bd69e48f1fdb92), [R19.anti-abuse](#q-b8ff396951eb36). | 141 | Example 2: German resident with foreign dividends taxed 15% abroad LF[243, 243] | If he had chosen the tariff under § 32d(6), § 32d(5) still governs the credit, applied to the additional tariff tax on the capital income. The foreign tax must first be reduced by any source-country refund entitlement. If 30% (EUR 3,000) were withheld but the same treaty limits source tax to 15%, only EUR 1,500 is creditable in this case and EUR 1,500 is reclaimed from the source country; the German tax cap does not turn treaty-refundable tax into a German credit. | fb85812a814d6ff90e1c8d17ca9a925ac0591034 LF112 | The person is taxed as a resident on their German income: Grundfreibetrag, special expenses, the EUR 36 lump sum and the resident deductions apply. Income that is not subject to German tax is left out of the base but raises the rate (progression, § 32b(1) no. 5 EStG): the tax rate is found on taxable income plus that foreign income, then applied to taxable income alone. Splitting needs § 1a. Foreign capital income is a special case: § 2(5b) EStG keeps capital income under § 32d(1) out of "income" wherever the law refers to it, and whether foreign interest or dividends of a § 1(3) applicant count in the 90% test and in the progression is not settled here (see Not yet confirmed). | Exact wording retained at current LF122, Step 4: the § 1(3) assessment instead  |
| fb85812a814d6ff90e1c8d17ca9a925ac0591034 LF116 | - Non-resident employees are in tax class I (§ 38b(1) no. 1(b) EStG), unless treated as fully liable under § 1(3) or § 1a. | Exact wording retained at current LF126, Wage tax for non-resident employees  |
| fb85812a814d6ff90e1c8d17ca9a925ac0591034 LF117 | - The employer's Betriebsstättenfinanzamt (the tax office for the place of business), not a residence office, sets and changes their wage tax data and issues the tax ID (§ 39(2) sentence 2 and § 39(3) EStG). | Exact wording retained at current LF127, Wage tax for non-resident employees  |
| fb85812a814d6ff90e1c8d17ca9a925ac0591034 LF118 | - A § 1(3) or § 1a employee with wage tax data set this way must file (§ 46(2) no. 7(b) EStG). An employee who wants § 1(3) treatment only in the assessment applies with a return (§ 46(2) no. 9), at the employer's Betriebsstättenfinanzamt. | Exact wording retained at current LF128, Wage tax for non-resident employees  |
| fb85812a814d6ff90e1c8d17ca9a925ac0591034 LF122 | - The German payer withholds when the payment is made: 15% of gross, 30% for supervisory board fees, plus 5.5% surcharge on the tax. Travel and hotel costs the payer refunds count as receipts only above actual cost and the per diem lump sums. | Exact wording retained at current LF132, § 50a withholding (artists, athletes, royalties, supervisory boards)  |
| fb85812a814d6ff90e1c8d17ca9a925ac0591034 LF123 | - For an EU/EEA creditor the payer may deduct costs the creditor proves, then withhold 30% (individual) or 15% (company) of the net (§ 50a(3)). | DE-L12 corrected statutory net-route scope, current LF64/127; royalties no3 excluded, actual eligible nationals/residence/company and proven direct-cost conditions retained.  |
| fb85812a814d6ff90e1c8d17ca9a925ac0591034 LF124 | - The payer files the withholding return with the BZSt by the 10th of the month after each calendar quarter and pays it over (§ 50a(5)). It must file even when nothing is withheld. The payer is liable for tax not withheld; the creditor can also be pursued. | Exact wording retained at current LF134, § 50a withholding (artists, athletes, royalties, supervisory boards)  |
| fb85812a814d6ff90e1c8d17ca9a925ac0591034 LF125 | - On request the payer issues a certificate of the tax withheld (§ 50a(5) sentence 7). The creditor needs it for a refund. | Exact wording retained at current LF135, § 50a withholding (artists, athletes, royalties, supervisory boards)  |
| fb85812a814d6ff90e1c8d17ca9a925ac0591034 LF129 | - The payer withholds in full even when a treaty limits German tax, unless the creditor holds an exemption certificate (Freistellungsbescheinigung) from the BZSt (§ 50c(1), (2)). For dividends, a treaty exemption certificate is given only to a company holding at least 10% of a German company. | Exact wording retained at current LF139, Treaty relief: exemption and refund (§ 50c EStG)  |
| fb85812a814d6ff90e1c8d17ca9a925ac0591034 LF130 | - Small royalties: where a treaty bars German tax and the year's royalties from that payer stay at or below EUR 10,000, the payer need not withhold (§ 50c(2) no. 2), but must still file the withholding return. | Exact wording retained at current LF140, Treaty relief: exemption and refund (§ 50c EStG)  |
| fb85812a814d6ff90e1c8d17ca9a925ac0591034 LF131 | - Refund: the creditor applies to the BZSt, electronically, within four years from the end of the year of receipt, with a residence certificate from their own tax authority and the payer's tax certificate (§ 50c(3), (5)). The BZSt warns that refunds can take more than 20 months. | Exact wording retained at current LF141, Treaty relief: exemption and refund (§ 50c EStG)  |
| fb85812a814d6ff90e1c8d17ca9a925ac0591034 LF132 | - Companies: treaty relief is denied to an interposed company whose owners would not get it directly and which has no real business of its own (§ 50d(3) EStG). | Exact wording retained at current LF142, Treaty relief: exemption and refund (§ 50c EStG) R19 replaces unconditional refund/automatic-denial wording with exact population, procedure, conjunctive anti-abuse and statutory exceptions. |
| fb85812a814d6ff90e1c8d17ca9a925ac0591034 LF136 | The German company withholds 25% plus 5.5% surcharge, 26.375% in all, whoever the shareholder is. For a non-resident shareholder without a German business, that settles the German tax. The excess over the treaty rate is reclaimed from the BZSt under § 50c(3). Example: a treaty limit of 15% gives a refund of 26.375% − 15% = 11.375% of the gross dividend. A foreign company that cannot use a treaty still gets two fifths of the tax back (§ 44a(9)). The treaty rate itself depends on the treaty; this skill has no rate table by country. | Full dividend-withholding/refund paragraph retained at current LF146; historical absent-country-table warning replaced by actual de-treaty-withholding-rates referral. R19 replaces unconditional refund/automatic-denial wording with exact population, procedure, conjunctive anti-abuse and statutory exceptions. |
| fb85812a814d6ff90e1c8d17ca9a925ac0591034 LF140 | Which rule applies depends on the income: | Exact wording retained at current LF150, Foreign tax credit for residents (§ 34c and § 32d(5) EStG)  |
| fb85812a814d6ff90e1c8d17ca9a925ac0591034 LF142 | - **Capital income under the flat tax (§ 32d(1)), such as foreign dividends and interest of a private investor.** § 34c does not apply (§ 34c(1) sentence 1, second half). The foreign tax is credited under § 32d(5): at most 25% of each item, and at most the German tax on the capital income. The flat tax is then (e − 4q) / 4 without church tax, where e is the capital income after the Sparer-Pauschbetrag and q the creditable foreign tax (§ 32d(1) sentence 4). Income that had no German Kapitalertragsteuer, for example through a foreign broker, is declared in the return (§ 32d(3)). | Exact wording retained at current LF152, Foreign tax credit for residents (§ 34c and § 32d(5) EStG)  |
| fb85812a814d6ff90e1c8d17ca9a925ac0591034 LF143 | - **Other foreign income taxed at the tariff (for example foreign rent, foreign business income, or capital income taxed at the tariff after the § 32d(6) option).** § 34c(1) applies where no treaty exists; where a treaty provides for a credit, § 34c(6) applies the same method. | fb85812a814d6ff90e1c8d17ca9a925ac0591034 LF167 | - **Late filing surcharge (§ 152 AO).** 0.25% per started month of the tax less prepayments and withholding, at least EUR 25 per month, at most EUR 25,000. | fb85812a814d6ff90e1c8d17ca9a925ac0591034 LF304 | Not yet confirmed: whether the BMF has issued a country-group list for 2026 (the 2 December 2025 letter applies from 2025 and no later one was found); how the tax office rounds the progression rate; whether foreign capital income of a § 1(3) applicant counts in the 90% test and in the progression, given § 2(5b) EStG; whether the § 50c(3) refund of dividend tax down to the treaty rate includes the solidarity surcharge (the example assumes it does); treaty withholding rates by country (no table here); church tax for non-residents (state law). | All original precise group/rounding/capital-test/surcharge/church reservations remain in current Sources; only the missing-country-table warning is replaced by the now-supplied treaty-rate sibling route at LF30/140.  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF18 | This skill decides whether a person is taxed in Germany on worldwide income or only on German income, then works out how the German income is taxed: by assessment, by wage tax, or by a flat withholding. It also covers the reverse case: a German resident with foreign income who wants credit for the foreign tax. | Exact wording retained at current LF18, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF20 | See also, and do not repeat here: | Exact wording retained at current LF20, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF22 | - **de-employee-income-tax**: the 2026 tariff, Werbungskosten and special expenses for a resident employee. The tariff formula there is the one used below. | Exact wording retained at current LF22, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF23 | - **de-rental-income**: how to compute the rental result (AfA, Werbungskosten, Anlage V). A non-resident landlord computes it the same way. | Exact wording retained at current LF23, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF24 | - **de-capital-gains**: the flat tax on investment income, the Sparer-Pauschbetrag and the loss pots. | Exact wording retained at current LF24, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF25 | - **de-payroll** and **germany-payroll**: running wage tax for an employee, including tax class I. | Exact wording retained at current LF25, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF26 | - **de-corporate-tax**: corporate tax at 15%, also for the German permanent establishment of a foreign company. | Exact wording retained at current LF26, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF27 | - **leaving-germany-exit-tax**: shareholders who move out, and extended limited liability after a move to a low-tax country (§ 2 AStG). | Exact wording retained at current LF27, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF28 | - **germany-to-switzerland-cross-border-tax**: Swiss commuters and moves to Switzerland. | Exact wording retained at current LF28, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF29 | - **de-treaty-withholding-rates**: treaty withholding rates by country, § 43b, § 50g and § 50d(3). | Exact wording retained at current LF29, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF33 | **Unlimited liability (§ 1(1) EStG).** A person with a residence (Wohnsitz) or habitual abode (gewöhnlicher Aufenthalt) in Germany is taxed on worldwide income. | Exact wording retained at current LF33, Who this applies to  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF35 | - **Residence (§ 8 AO):** a home the person keeps and uses, in circumstances showing they will keep and use it. A flat kept available in Germany is enough, even if the person spends most of the year abroad. | Exact wording retained at current LF35, Who this applies to  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF36 | - **Habitual abode (§ 9 AO):** staying in a way that shows the stay is not just temporary. A continuous stay of more than six months always counts, from the first day; short breaks are ignored. The six-month rule does not apply to a purely private stay (visit, holiday, cure) of up to one year (§ 9 sentence 3 AO). | Exact wording retained at current LF36, Who this applies to  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF37 | - **German state employees abroad (§ 1(2) EStG):** German nationals paid from a German public purse, taxed abroad only like non-residents, stay fully liable. | Exact wording retained at current LF37, Who this applies to  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF39 | **Limited liability (§ 1(4) EStG).** A person with neither residence nor habitual abode in Germany is taxed only on German-source income listed in § 49 EStG. | Exact wording retained at current LF39, Who this applies to  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF41 | **The § 1(3) option.** A non-resident can apply to be treated as fully liable for their German income if, in the calendar year, either: | Exact wording retained at current LF41, Who this applies to  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF43 | - at least 90% of their total income is subject to German income tax, or | Exact wording retained at current LF43, Who this applies to  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF44 | - the income not subject to German tax is no more than the Grundfreibetrag, EUR 12,348 for 2026. The amount is cut to ¾, ½ or ¼ for residents of lower-cost countries (BMF country groups; Austria and most of Western Europe are in group 1, full amount). | Exact wording retained at current LF44, Who this applies to  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF46 | German income that a tax treaty limits to a reduced rate (for example dividends) counts as not subject to German tax for the test (§ 1(3) sentence 3). The foreign income must be shown by a certificate from the foreign tax authority (§ 1(3) sentence 5). § 50a withholding still applies (§ 1(3) sentence 6). | Exact wording retained at current LF46, Who this applies to  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF48 | **§ 1a EStG, EU/EEA nationals.** An EU or EEA national who is fully liable under § 1(1) or treated so under § 1(3) can have a spouse who lives in another EU/EEA state treated as fully liable, so the couple gets joint assessment and splitting. For the § 1(3) test the income of both spouses counts and the Grundfreibetrag is doubled to EUR 24,696 (§ 1a(1) no. 2). A non-EU/EEA national under § 1(3) gets the Grundfreibetrag but not splitting. | Exact wording retained at current LF48, Who this applies to  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF50 | **Companies.** A company with neither its seat nor its place of management in Germany has limited corporate tax liability on its German income (§ 2 no. 1 KStG). Income settled by withholding is final unless it belongs to a German business (§ 32(1) KStG). A German permanent establishment is assessed at the 15% corporate rate (§ 23 KStG; see de-corporate-tax). | Exact wording retained at current LF50, Who this applies to R20 preserves final withholding subject to the complete stated statutory assessment gates. |

| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF116 | The person is taxed as a resident on their German income: Grundfreibetrag, special expenses, the EUR 36 lump sum and the resident deductions apply. Income that is not subject to German tax is left out of the base but raises the rate (progression, § 32b(1) no. 5 EStG): the tax rate is found on taxable income plus that foreign income, then applied to taxable income alone. Splitting needs § 1a. Foreign capital income is a special case: § 2(5b) EStG keeps capital income under § 32d(1) out of "income" wherever the law refers to it, and whether foreign interest or dividends of a § 1(3) applicant count in the 90% test and in the progression is not settled here (see Not yet confirmed). | Exact wording retained at current LF122, Step 4: the § 1(3) assessment instead  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF120 | - Non-resident employees are in tax class I (§ 38b(1) no. 1(b) EStG), unless treated as fully liable under § 1(3) or § 1a. | Exact wording retained at current LF126, Wage tax for non-resident employees  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF121 | - The employer's Betriebsstättenfinanzamt (the tax office for the place of business), not a residence office, sets and changes their wage tax data and issues the tax ID (§ 39(2) sentence 2 and § 39(3) EStG). | Exact wording retained at current LF127, Wage tax for non-resident employees  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF122 | - A § 1(3) or § 1a employee with wage tax data set this way must file (§ 46(2) no. 7(b) EStG). An employee who wants § 1(3) treatment only in the assessment applies with a return (§ 46(2) no. 9), at the employer's Betriebsstättenfinanzamt. | Exact wording retained at current LF128, Wage tax for non-resident employees  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF126 | - The German payer withholds when the payment is made: 15% of gross, 30% for supervisory board fees, plus 5.5% surcharge on the tax. Travel and hotel costs the payer refunds count as receipts only above actual cost and the per diem lump sums. | Exact wording retained at current LF132, § 50a withholding (artists, athletes, royalties, supervisory boards)  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF127 | - For an EU/EEA creditor the payer may deduct costs the creditor proves, then withhold 30% (individual) or 15% (company) of the net (§ 50a(3)). | DE-L12 corrected statutory net-route scope, current LF64/127; royalties no3 excluded, actual eligible nationals/residence/company and proven direct-cost conditions retained.  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF128 | - The payer files the withholding return with the BZSt by the 10th of the month after each calendar quarter and pays it over (§ 50a(5)). | 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF129 | - On request the payer issues a certificate of the tax withheld (§ 50a(5) sentence 7). The creditor needs it for a refund. | Exact wording retained at current LF135, § 50a withholding (artists, athletes, royalties, supervisory boards)  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF133 | - The payer withholds in full even when a treaty limits German tax, unless the creditor holds an exemption certificate (Freistellungsbescheinigung) from the BZSt (§ 50c(1), (2)). For dividends, a treaty exemption certificate is given only to a company holding at least 10% of a German company. | Exact wording retained at current LF139, Treaty relief: exemption and refund (§ 50c EStG)  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF134 | - Small royalties: where a treaty bars German tax and the year's royalties from that payer stay at or below EUR 10,000, the payer need not withhold (§ 50c(2) no. 2), but must still file the withholding return. | Exact wording retained at current LF140, Treaty relief: exemption and refund (§ 50c EStG)  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF135 | - Refund: the creditor applies to the BZSt, electronically, within four years from the end of the year of receipt, with a residence certificate from their own tax authority and the payer's tax certificate (§ 50c(3), (5)). The BZSt warns that refunds can take more than 20 months. | Exact wording retained at current LF141, Treaty relief: exemption and refund (§ 50c EStG)  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF136 | - Companies: treaty relief is denied to an interposed company whose owners would not get it directly and which has no real business of its own (§ 50d(3) EStG). | Exact wording retained at current LF142, Treaty relief: exemption and refund (§ 50c EStG) R19 replaces unconditional refund/automatic-denial wording with exact population, procedure, conjunctive anti-abuse and statutory exceptions. |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF140 | The German company withholds 25% plus 5.5% surcharge, 26.375% in all, whoever the shareholder is. For a non-resident shareholder without a German business, that settles the German tax. The excess over the treaty rate is reclaimed from the BZSt under § 50c(3). Example: a treaty limit of 15% gives a refund of 26.375% − 15% = 11.375% of the gross dividend. A foreign company that cannot use a treaty still gets two fifths of the tax back (§ 44a(9)). The treaty rate itself depends on the treaty; see de-treaty-withholding-rates for the rates by country. | Exact wording retained at current LF146, Kapitalertragsteuer on dividends to non-residents R19 replaces unconditional refund/automatic-denial wording with exact population, procedure, conjunctive anti-abuse and statutory exceptions. |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF144 | Which rule applies depends on the income: | Exact wording retained at current LF150, Foreign tax credit for residents (§ 34c and § 32d(5) EStG)  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF146 | - **Capital income under the flat tax (§ 32d(1)), such as foreign dividends and interest of a private investor.** § 34c does not apply (§ 34c(1) sentence 1, second half). The foreign tax is credited under § 32d(5): at most 25% of each item, and at most the German tax on the capital income. The flat tax is then (e − 4q) / 4 without church tax, where e is the capital income after the Sparer-Pauschbetrag and q the creditable foreign tax (§ 32d(1) sentence 4). Income that had no German Kapitalertragsteuer, for example through a foreign broker, is declared in the return (§ 32d(3)). | Exact wording retained at current LF152, Foreign tax credit for residents (§ 34c and § 32d(5) EStG)  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF147 | - **Other foreign income taxed at the tariff (for example foreign rent, foreign business income, or capital income taxed at the tariff after the § 32d(6) option).** § 34c(1) applies where no treaty exists; where a treaty provides for a credit, § 34c(6) applies the same method. The foreign tax is credited up to the German tax on that income: | DE-L11 corrected own32d(6) credit routing/refund-first illustration, current foreign-credit section and Example2; original mistaken34c inclusion/refundable2250 illustration not retained as correct.  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF149 |   credit cap = German tariff tax × foreign income from that country / taxable income (sentence 2: the average rate on the whole taxable income, applied to the foreign income). | Exact wording retained at current LF155, Foreign tax credit for residents (§ 34c and § 32d(5) EStG)  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF151 |   Foreign tax above the cap is lost. Only tax that was set, paid and reduced by any refund due abroad counts. Instead of the credit the taxpayer can deduct the foreign tax from the income (§ 34c(2)). | Exact wording retained at current LF157, Foreign tax credit for residents (§ 34c and § 32d(5) EStG)  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF152 | - **Income a treaty exempts in Germany** (often foreign employment income or foreign rent): no credit, but it raises the rate under § 32b(1) no. 3. Exception: rent from property in another EU/EEA state, and profits of an EU/EEA permanent establishment that fails the active-business test of § 2a(2), do not raise the rate (§ 32b(1) sentence 2). | Exact wording retained at current LF158, Foreign tax credit for residents (§ 34c and § 32d(5) EStG)  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF154 | What counts as foreign income is set out in § 34d EStG. | Exact wording retained at current LF160, Foreign tax credit for residents (§ 34c and § 32d(5) EStG)  |

| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF171 | - **Late filing surcharge (§ 152 AO).** 0.25% per started month of the tax less prepayments and withholding, at least EUR 25 per month, at most EUR 25,000. It is compulsory if the return is not filed within 14 months after the end of the year (for 2026: by 29 February 2028), unless an extension was granted, the tax is nil or negative, or the tax does not exceed prepayments and withholding (§ 152(2), (3) AO). | Exact wording retained at current LF177, Penalties and interest  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF172 | - **Interest (§ 233a, § 238 AO).** 0.15% per full month (1.8% a year) on the balance, from 15 months after year end: from 1 April 2028 for 2026. | Exact wording retained at current LF178, Penalties and interest  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF173 | - **Late payment (§ 240 AO).** 1% per started month on the overdue tax rounded down to EUR 50. | Exact wording retained at current LF179, Penalties and interest  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF174 | - **§ 50a payer.** The payer is liable for tax it did not withhold or pay over (§ 50a(5) sentence 5). The creditor can also be pursued. | Exact wording retained at current LF180, Penalties and interest  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF178 | - **Treaties override.** A treaty can take away Germany's right to tax (for example employment income under the 183-day rule) or cap it (dividends, royalties). Withholding still happens first; relief is by exemption certificate or refund (§ 50c). | Exact wording retained at current LF184, Cross-border points  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF179 | - **Moving in or out during the year.** A person who is fully liable for part of the year is assessed once; foreign income of the non-resident months raises the rate (§ 2(7), § 32b(1) no. 2 EStG). | 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF180 | - **Leaving with shares.** See leaving-germany-exit-tax, including extended limited liability under § 2 AStG after a move to a low-tax country. | Exact wording retained at current LF186, Cross-border points  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF181 | - **VAT.** A non-resident letting German property or performing in Germany may also have German VAT duties. Not covered here; see germany-vat-return. | Exact wording retained at current LF187, Cross-border points  |

| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF188 | Anna lives in Vienna and has no home in Germany. Her German flat produces a rental result of EUR 24,000 for 2026, computed as in de-rental-income. Her only other income is a rental result of EUR 2,000 from a garage she lets in Vienna. (Invented case.) | Exact wording retained at current LF194, Example 1: Austrian resident with a German rental flat  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF190 | **Limited liability (default).** No personal deductions, no EUR 36 lump sum. Taxable income EUR 24,000. The tariff is applied to 24,000 + 12,348 = 36,348. z = (36,348 − 17,799) / 10,000 = 1.8549. Tax = (173.10 × 1.8549 + 2,397) × 1.8549 + 1,034.87 = 6,076.65, rounded down EUR 6,076. Below EUR 20,350, so no surcharge. | Exact wording retained at current LF196, Example 1: Austrian resident with a German rental flat  |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF192 | **§ 1(3) option.** German income is 24,000 / 26,000 = 92.3% of her total, and the Austrian income is under EUR 12,348 (Austria is in country group 1), so both tests pass. She needs a certificate from the Austrian tax office for the EUR 2,000. Taxable income 24,000 − 36 = EUR 23,964. The Austrian rent is not subject to German tax, so it raises the rate (§ 32b(1) no. 5; the EU/EEA rent exception in § 32b(1) sentence 2 covers only no. 3): tax on 23,964 + 2,000 = 25,964 is EUR 3,107, a rate of 11.9666%. Tax = 23,964 × 11.9666% = EUR 2,867. The option saves EUR 3,209. | Exact wording retained at current LF198, Example 1: Austrian resident with a German rental flat  |
